岐路に立つ米国AI投資ブーム
～持続に向けた必要条件と目先のリスク点検～
2026年10月29日
みずほ総合研究所
調査部
【みずほ経済インサイト】

### Page 2

 AI市場の成長期待を背景に、競争優位の確保に向けた巨額投資と、それに伴うAI技術の高
度化が進展
 投資資金の確保に向けて、社債発行など外部資金調達が活発化。こうした資金需要の拡大
が金利上昇の一因となり、企業の資金調達コストは増大
 AI関連の投資競争が続くなか、AIブームは企業の選別・再編が進む新たな局面へ。その過程
で経営資源が競争力の高い企業に引き継がれ、AI市場全体の成長は継続すると予想
 性能向上と利用コストの低下により、AIの活用可能な領域は拡大する見込み。ユーザー企業
間の競争がAIの普及を加速させ、市場の持続的な成長を後押し
 AI市場の中核を担う企業の財務は健全。金融システム全体が不安定化するリスクは限定的
 一方、AIインフラ運営企業は将来的な財務負担が高まりやすい構造。特にネオクラウドでは、
減価償却費の負担が利益を圧迫するほか、2030年頃には設備更新や債務借換えが資金繰
りの重荷に。市場による企業評価は一段と厳格化し、投融資先の選別が進む可能性
要旨

（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
巨額のAI投資は金融環境にどのような変化をもたらしているか？（P4～6）ポイント①
AI開発・インフラ企業の脆弱性はどこに・どの程度あるのか？（P7～10）ポイント②
AIブームは持続するのか？（P11～12）ポイント③

AIを巡る競争は白熱。AIの性能向上と巨額投資が続く
 AI市場の成長期待が高まるなか、先端AI企業間の技術開発・投資競争に拍車がかかる
― AIモデルの高度化が進み、性能指標は過去数年で飛躍的に上昇
― 競争優位の確保に向けた巨額の設備投資が継続。AI関連企業の投資は2026年時点で1.2兆ドルに到達し、アナリスト
は、今後も高水準で推移すると予想

企業の資金余力は潤沢も、巨額の投資に備え更なる資金確保の動き
 AI投資は過熱感を帯びつつあるものの、資金枯渇によるクラッシュの可能性は低い
― 米国非金融法人は投資増を上回る内部資金の増加によりマクロで資金余剰を維持。投資継続を支えるバッファーは十分
― ITバブル期は企業部門が資金不足に傾くなかで投資を拡大したが、今回は内部資金が潤沢
 もっとも、米国非金融法人の外部資金調達（社債発行・銀行借入等）は活発化
― 調達増は内部資金不足の補填というより、巨額の先行投資に備えた更なる資金確保を反映している可能性

AI市場を担う主要企業の財務基盤は健全。 AIインフラ運営企業は財務負担が高まりやすい構造
 AI市場をけん引するハイパースケーラー、半導体開発・製造企業の財務基盤は比較的健全
― これらの企業はAI関連企業の売上高の78.8％、債務の55.0％を占めているため、直ちに金融システム全体の不安定化
につながるとは考えにくい
 一方、データセンター運営やネオクラウド（GPUサーバー運営）などのAIインフラ運営企業は財務負担が高まりやすい構造

― これらの企業では、投資回収の遅れや資金調達環境の悪化により財務リスクが顕在化するおそれ
（注） 1. ハイパースケーラーは、大規模なクラウド基盤を運営する巨大IT企業。ネオクラウドは、GPU（AIの学習・推論などに用いる半導体）サーバーを運用し、計算資源を提供する企業
2. 電力供給企業など表に記載していない分類があるため、構成比の合計は100％とならない
（出所） LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

ネオクラウドの減価償却負担の増加が、利益改善と株式評価の重荷に
 AIインフラ運営企業のうち、特にネオクラウドでは、先行投資に伴う減価償却費の大幅な増加が見込まれる。売上拡大に
よって償却負担を吸収できるかが、今後の利益改善を左右
 利益改善が市場予想を下回れば、株式評価の悪化も
― GPUの急速な技術進歩による早期陳腐化も懸念材料。利益の黒字化が市場予想より遅れた場合には、株価下落や増

資条件の悪化につながる可能性

＜発行＞ ＜償還＞
満期到来で
資金需要増
巨額投資に伴うFCF赤字が続くなか、設備更新と債務償還 の重なりが資金繰りを圧迫する懸念
 巨額の先行投資を背景に、ネオクラウドのフリーキャッシュフロー（FCF、営業キャッシュフローから設備投資額を差し引いて
計算）は、今後数年間の赤字が見込まれる。手元資金が減少し、追加資金調達の必要性が高まる可能性
 設備更新と債務償還の重なりが資金繰りの負担に
― GPUの耐用年数は概ね5～6年。また、ネオクラウドが発行する社債の年限は5年程度。十分な資金を確保できなければ、

投資の縮小や事業拡大の足踏みにつながるリスク

完値を含む
2. 各年は年度ベースで表記しており、決算期の違いにより対象期間にずれがある

### Page 10

2030
年頃に更新投資が必要に
AIインフラ運営企業の収益性に対する評価は、数年以内により厳しくなる見込み
 AIインフラ運営企業は今後数年以内に収益化を果たせるかが焦点。市場では2030年頃のリスクを見据え選別が進む見込み
― 経営への懸念が強まるネオクラウドは、フリーキャッシュフローの赤字が続く見込み。減価償却負担の増加が利益改善の
重荷となるなか、2030年頃には債務の借換えや半導体の更新投資が重なり、資金繰りが厳しくなる懸念
― 半導体の性能向上や価格低下により、既存設備の競争力が低下し、投資回収が遅れるリスクも
ネオクラウドのリスクを測る注目指標

AI投資ブームはまだ入口。「終わりの始まり」ではなく、「始まりの終わり」
 AI投資ブームは、競争激化に伴い企業の選別・再編が進む新たな局面へ
― マクロでみた設備過剰感は初期段階。今後もモデル開発や計算基盤を巡る競争が激化するなか、資金の出し手や事業
パートナーによる選別は一段と厳格化。一部企業では経営破綻や事業再編が進む可能性
― もっとも、個別企業の淘汰はAI技術の衰退を意味せず。設備・顧客・人材などの経営資源が競争力の高い企業へ再配
分されることで、AIの発展・普及は続く見込み

（注） 1994～2000年は2002年当時の公表値。2026年は見込み値
（出所） 米国商務省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
米国：IT機器に関するストック循環図 AI関連企業の選別と再編（イメージ）
マクロの視点では設備過剰感が出るのはこれか ら
11
（出所）みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
投資意欲が強い
設備過剰感が強い
ITブーム時は設備過剰感か ら
急激な投資選別が進行
設備・顧客・人材が再配分され、
AIエコシステムは発展継続

31 過去2週間でAIを利用した
今後6カ月以内でAIを利用する
（回答割合、％）
（年/月）
AIの普及は不可逆な潮流。技術進歩に加え、ユーザー企業間の競争が導入を促進
 技術進歩によるAI導入の経済的メリット拡大と、ユーザー企業間の競争圧力がAI実装を後押し
― 性能向上や利用コストの低下によって導入のハードルが下がり、AI活用のすそ野は拡大
― AIを活用する企業が増えるなか、業界内の競争に取り残されないためにAI導入の必要性が高まる
 AIの利用実績・利用意向はともに上昇傾向にあり、今後も企業への普及が進む見込み
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

固定收益 | 证券研究报告 — 点评报告 2026 年 11 月 12 日

油价影响发散、 房价抑制渐弱

2026 年 10 月美国 CPI 数据点评   
近期中、美股债市场应重点关注的因素仍是 AI 叙事和能源价格。 
 美国 10 月 CPI 数据因同比指标缺失，对通胀趋势的指示性相对有限，但
环比涨幅超预期说明通胀压力仍然明显。 其中，房租和食品价格上涨是
10 月 CPI 涨幅超预期的主要推动力 。 
 我们认为，美国 10 月食品价格加速上行，可能在一定程度上受到了油价
上涨的外溢性影响。10 月美国食品 CPI 与柴油价格的比价，已再次接近
“次贷危机”以来的低点。油价可能增加了美国农业生产的成本， 进而
向下游的食品价格传导压力。 
 类似的逻辑对更广泛的美国物价上涨可能也有解释力。除食品价格外，
核心 CPI 与油价的比价也处于多年来的较低水平，这种比价效应也对更
广泛的商品、服务价格有所推动。 
 美国 10 月房租上涨可能反映房价周期对通胀的抑制作用已经减弱 。 一般
而言，美国房价周期领先于房租周期 1 到 2 年，从成屋中位售价的同比
涨幅来看，美国房价周期大约在 2023 年 5 月前后就已触底，随后反弹。
因此，当前美国房租涨幅有所回升是正常现象。 
 尽管美联储加息对美国房价周期产生了一定的抑制作用，但美国房价在
经历疫情后一波显著上涨之后，事实上回调比较有限。 通常房地产周期
作为重要的周期驱动因素 ，对投资周期和经济周期的拐点应该有比较强
的塑造作用，但由于近年来 AI 相关产业投资的带动，且 AI 叙事逻辑在
短期内受到利率影响又比较有限，所以美联储即使大幅度加息，对美国
经济周期的抑制效果截至目前也不够明显。 
 我们仍然认为，AI 叙事和能源价格是近期我国金融市场需要重点关注的
因素(《两种逻辑的碰撞》 20261108)，对美国金融市场也是如此。其中
AI 叙事的影响在短期内更直接 。在美国 10 月 CPI 数据公布当日，费城
半导体指数受其影响不明显，再次说明利率在短期内不是 AI 叙事的主要
关注点， 产业逻辑本身更加重要。 如果美股AI 概念科技股抛售告一段落，
美债、中债近期的交易窗口可能随之结束。 
 反之，如果市场担忧 AI 叙事进一步动摇，即使美国通胀暂时仍在高位，
中期内美债市场也仍有交易机会 。 
 我们认为，油价对美国通胀的影响有 发散迹象，这会进一步强化民主党
在国会换届后限制美伊冲突的动机。而在中期选举结果较为悬殊背景下，
特朗普政府对 伊朗采取激进策略的可能性不大，油价对美国通胀和金融
市场的影响趋于缓和的概率较高，美伊局势失控的概率有限 。 
 风险提示： 国内外实体经济出现超预期变化；国内政策超预期收紧；国
际地缘政治紧张局势升级等。

买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上；

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数；

Document 099: 2028年——美国中选给AI上游供应链“挖的一个坑”

Source PDF: all report pdf/2028年——美国中选给AI上游供应链“挖的一个坑”.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

⼤摩担忧，蓝⾊浪潮后，数据中⼼项⽬延迟可能在 2028 年造成科技供应链的 “ 空档期 ” （ air
pocket ），引发 “ 定价权下降或库存积压 ” ，甚⾄出现订单放缓、利润率正常化和盈利下调，
即便 AI 需求本⾝仍然完整。亚洲云半导体⼚商以及旧内存板块评级遭集体下调，理由是定
价权 “ ⻅顶 ” 预期或提前到来。
摘要：
⼀场选举，让华尔街重新审视 AI 建设节奏。
2026 年 11 ⽉，美国中期选举结果出炉。⺠主党不仅拿下国会两院，还在德州、俄亥俄州赢得州
⻓席位 —— 这两个州恰好是美国数据中⼼建设最密集的地区。
摩根⼠丹利最新研报《 Buy the Drop: AI Investment Opportunities Post the US Midterms 》判
断，美国蓝⾊浪潮后，美国数据中⼼建设将有约 5GW 容量从 2028 年推迟⾄ 2029 年，相当于美国
当年新增容量的 20% 、全球新增容量的 10% 。
这⼀推迟带来的直接结果是：美国数据中⼼ GW 新增容量 2028 年同⽐下降 4% ， 2029 年反弹

38% ；全球层⾯， 2028 年同⽐增⻓ 9% ， 2029 年同⽐增⻓ 20% 。
美国中选似乎也给 AI 上游供应链 “ 挖了⼀个坑 ”—— 它的名字叫 2028 年。⼤摩担忧，⼤量数据中⼼
推迟“ 可能在 2028 年造成科技供应链的空档期 'air pocket' ，导致定价权削弱或库存积累。甚⾄
出现订单放缓、利润率正常化和盈利下调，即便 AI 需求本⾝仍然完整。 ”
对于 2028 年 “ 空档期 ” 的影响，上游云半导体⾸当其冲。分析师随即将亚洲云半导体供应商
Aspeed 等⽬标价⼤幅下调，理由是未来 2-4 个季度⾯临美国项⽬延迟⻛险；旧内存板块同样被下
调，理由是定价权 “ ⻅顶 ” 预期提前到来。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

美国数据中⼼累计容量路径图：显⽰ 2028 年从 110GW 下调⾄ 105GW
为什么是 2028 ？选举如何挖坑
数据中⼼推迟的直接触发点来⾃美国中期选举。
⺠主党赢得了包括德克萨斯州和俄亥俄州在内的多个州⻓职位。其中德克萨斯州当选州⻓ Gina
Hinojosa 的竞选纲领包括：在德州⽴法机构制定更强保护措施之前，暂停新数据中⼼审批。俄亥
俄州当选州⻓ Amy Acton 则提出有条件暂停新数据中⼼和新许可，要求项⽬⽀付 100% 的天然
⽓、⽔和电⼒成本，并使⽤⼯会劳⼯。
分析师对此的判断是： 2027 年在建项⽬基本不受影响，因为 “ 新当选州⻓不太可能破坏已在建设
中的项⽬ ” 。延迟⻛险集中在尚未开⼯的 2028 年项⽬。
报告指出，德克萨斯州和俄亥俄州此前在 2028 年的数据中⼼容量敞⼝合计约 4.9GW （ IT ⼝径）。
这批项⽬因新的审批流程⽽⾯临后移。
与此同时， ERCOT （德州电⽹）的互联申请已达约 474GW ，超过其历史峰值⽤电需求的 5 倍以
上，其中数据中⼼约占新增电⼒请求的 90% 。电⼒瓶颈早已存在，选举结果只是在此基础上叠

加了政策不确定性。
上游供应链的 “ 坑 ” ：云半导体⾸当其冲
2028 年这个空档期对 AI 上游供应链意味着什么？

### Page 3

⼤摩的亚洲半导体团队迅速跟进发布报告《 Cloud Semis: Peak Growth, Less Upside 》，点出：
云半导体“Peak Growth, Less Upside” （增⻓⻅顶，上⾏空间有限）。
Aspeed 是服务器主板管理控制器（ BMC ）的核⼼供应商，直接受益于数据中⼼扩建周期。数据
中⼼半导体公司 Montage ，核⼼产品是服务器 DRAM 的内存互联芯⽚。
两家公司的⽬标价均⼤幅下调：
Aspeed ：⽬标价从 NT$24,000 降⾄ NT$14,417 ，降幅约 40%
Montage ：⽬标价从 HK$432 降⾄ HK$207 ，降幅约 52%
分析师解释称，降级的核⼼逻辑是：“ 美国团队预计约 20% 的 2028 年美国数据中⼼新增容量可能
转移⾄ 2029 年，可能影响云半导体供应商的 2028 年盈利。 ”
旧内存的 “ 定价权⻅顶 ” ：⽐想象来得更早
同期，⼤摩旧内存研究团队发布报告《 Old Memory: Peak Pricing Power? Reduce exposure. 》

（旧内存：定价权⻅顶？减少敞⼝），将 Winbond 、 GigaDevice 、 Powerchip 、 Macronix 均从超
配下调⾄中性， Phison 更被下调⾄低配。
报告明确说明，分析范围仅限于 DDR3/4 、 SLC/MLC NAND 和 NOR ，不涉及主流 DDR5 或
TLC/QLC NAND 市场。
核⼼判断是：“ 旧内存的定价优势已不再⾜够。 ”
报告列出三重压⼒：
云端⽀出可⻅度恶化
消费需求疲软
供需到 2028 年趋于正常化
其中最直接的触发因素，正是美国数据中⼼建设节奏的后移。“2028 年科技供应链可能出现空档
期，可能导致定价权下降和库存积压。 ”

### Page 4

报告特别强调，这些公司的 2026 年 EPS 实际上仍在上调，降级的核⼼是 “ 周期拐点管理 ” ⽽⾮盈
利崩塌。
⼤云⼚商：资本开⽀不变，但节奏错位
尽管项⽬延迟，摩根⼠丹利维持对超⼤规模云⼚商的资本开⽀预测：⾕歌 /Alphabet 2027/2028
年合计 3750 亿 /4000 亿美元， Meta 2250 亿 /2500 亿美元，亚⻢逊 3550 亿 /3650 亿美元，微软
2380 亿 /2980 亿美元，均保持不变。
报告认为，这些公司仍会 “ 尽可能多地建设容量 ” ，因为⼤语⾔模型⽀持的新产品⾯向数⼗亿⽤
⼾，且仍有超过 30% 的资本回报率。
但 “ 花多少钱 ” 和 “ 何时建成 ” 是两回事。
报告指出，延迟的影响更多是 “ 推迟⽽⾮取消 ” 。部分项⽬可能从德州、俄亥俄州转移到更友好的
州，如⽥纳西州、路易斯安那州。海外⽅⾯，摩根⼠丹利预测可能有20GW 的潜在溢出需求流向
亚洲和欧洲中东⾮洲地区。
Meta 表⽰⽬标在 2027 年实现10 倍算⼒效率提升；⾕歌的 TPU 8t 和 8i 相⽐上⼀代分别提供最⾼ 2.7

倍的训练性能提升和80% 的推理性价⽐提升。
这些效率进步被视为应对容量约束的缓冲⼯具。
硅⾕的信⼼  vs 华尔街的谨慎
近期紧张局⾯不必⽴即消失，股票就可以开始重新定价：短缺收窄就⾜以在盈利⻅顶前削弱增量
定价权。

### Page 5

对于这场 2028 年的 “ 空档期 ” ，市场并⾮只有⼀种声⾳。
投资⼈ Puru Saxena （ AlphaTarget 创始⼈）在社交媒体上发⽂，提供了⼀个与摩根⼠丹利基本
⼀致但更简洁的判断：“ ⺠主党中期选举⼤胜确实引发了关于 AI 数据中⼼建设的合理疑问，但现
实⽐近期市场反应更为复杂。 ”
他指出， 2027 年的容量 “ 基本完好 ” ，因为多数明年上线的项⽬已在建设中，新当选州⻓预计不会
中断已启动的⼯程。
“AI 基础设施周期正在被拉⻓，⽽不是终结。 ” 他写道，并援引投资格⾔： “ 在别⼈恐惧时贪婪是值
得的。 ”
这与摩根⼠丹利的核⼼⽴场⼀致 —— 但华尔街的分析更为细化：供应链不同环节、不同地理位
置、不同资本结构的公司，所⾯临的⻛险截然不同。
已建成、已获电⼒保障的数据中⼼资产稀缺性将上升；融资质量分化将加深； neoclouds 和新建
项⽬将⾯临更严格的审查，⽽⼤型云⼚商凭借规模和资本实⼒将进⼀步巩固优势。⼤摩称：
2028 年的坑，谁来填
⼤摩的整个分析框架指向⼀个核⼼结论：AI 需求没有消失，但建设节奏的错位将在 2028 年制造

⼀个真实的供应链压⼒窗⼝。
融资仍然可得，但中期选举应会加深质量分化。

### Page 6

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

图⽚
这个 ” 坑 “ 不是由技术失败挖出的，⽽是由⼀场州级选举、⼀批新的许可要求、以及 474GW 积压
的 ERCOT 并⽹申请共同造成的。
电⼒ —— ⽽⾮芯⽚ —— 正在成为这场 AI 竞赛的新瓶颈。
报告最终建议投资者关注三类机会：⼤型云⼚商、精选数据中⼼供应链标的，以及软件使能者
和 AI 应⽤采⽤者。
我们认为市场的反应过度，将部分项⽬从 2028 年推迟⾄ 2029 年，⽽不是破坏资本开⽀的整体逻
辑。

Hon Hai (2317.TW): Oct in line: revenues +46% YoY; New AI servers, CPO 
switch and Foldable phones to drive 4Q26; Buy (on CL)
6 November 2026 | 1:57PM HKT
Hon Hai’s Oct revenues were +46% YoY/ 13% MoM to NT$1.3tn, or 4% higher than 
our estimates, reflecting strong AI servers ramp up and new smartphone models in 
contribution. From a MoM perspective, Consumer electronics was the strongest, 
followed by Computing, Components, and Cloud and Networking. Consumer 
electronics, Computing and Components segments are positive MoM, while Cloud 
and Networking saw slight decline MoM, due to the shift by consignment model, 
tight supply, and early-stage production ramps of new models, despite the fact that 
AI-related demand remained strong. The strong MoM of the other 3 segments 
reaffirms our positive view on the company’s leading market position across: (1) 
Smartphone: serving global leading brands, which suffer less under high memory 
costs, with new model launches and form factor changes (report link), driving the 
shipment and ASP increase, (2) AI servers: serving global leading CSP customers, 
and early in the delivery of the latest AI server racks, driving the shipment and ASP 
increase. The company is also the major supplier of CPO switch, supporting 4Q26 
growth. From a YoY perspective, Cloud and Networking was the strongest, followed 
by Consumer electronics, Components and Computing, and all segments were 
positive YoY. We expect to see both YoY and QoQ growth throughout 4Q26, driven 
by new AI server racks, new smartphone models, and CPO switches.

3-month revenues preview: We expect company’s 4Q26E revenues to be +40% 
YoY/ +21% QoQ to NT$3.7 trillion. 3Q26 delivered strong revenues YoY growth of 
47%, or 5% / 7% ahead of our / Bloomberg consensus, and we expect to see both 
YoY and QoQ growth throughout 4Q26, driven by new AI server racks, new

Earnings revision: We factor in Hon Hai Oct revenues and revised down 2026E net 
income by 5%, mainly on lower revenues due to weak consumer electronics demand. We 
remain positive on AI server racks ramp up, Hon Hai’s leading market position in terms of 
scale, technology, and execution, and rising foldable phones to drive growth ahead. We 
keep our 2027-28E estimates largely unchanged.

### Page 3

Valuation: Our 12m TP for Hon Hai is unchanged at NT$400 and we continue to derive it 
through near-term P/E. Our target 2027E P/E multiple of 17.9x (unchanged) is derived 
from peers’ average ratio of trading P/E to forward year NI YoY growth and OPM (P/E 
divided by the sum of NI YoY and OPM). Our target P/E of 17.9x is in the range of the 
company’s historical forward P/E since 2010, reflecting our positive view on Hon Hai’s 
business expansion from the competitive consumer electronics market to AI servers and 
EVs outsourcing, which should drive profit expansion with diversifying business 
opportunities for Hon Hai. Maintain Buy (on AP AC CL).

Price Target Risks and Methodology – Hon Hai 
Valuation methodology: We are Buy rated on Hon Hai. Our 12-month target price of 
NT$400 is based on an 17.9x 2027E P/E multiple, which is set in line with peers’ PEG&M 
ratio (i.e., P/E vs. forward-year earnings growth and OPM). 
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected ramp-up of the AI server business; (2) 
weaker-than-expected EV total solution performance across EV assembly, design,

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
In-line 3Q26 results
Co-Tech reported 3Q26 results:
• 3Q revenue was NT$3,169mn (+13% q/q, +60% y/y).
• GM was 26.7% (-70bps q/q, +620bps y/y), 20/150bps below MSe/Street,
which we attributed to higher copper pricing and electricity rates (link).
• OP came in at NT$757mn (+11% q/q, +125% y/y), 1% above MSe but 5% below
the Street, for OpM of 23.9% (-50bps q/q, +690bps y/y), 30bps above MSe
but 140bps below the Street.

• As a result, NP was NT$608mn (+12% q/q, +140% y/y), in line with MSe but
4% below the Street, for EPS of NT$2.41.
• Co-Tech’s board also approved up to NT$7bn of CBs to fund the company's
Malaysian expansion. Issue price will be set by auction. Conversion price is
TBD pending regulatory approval.
• Reiterate OW; PT NT$745.
See also our initiation report: Asia Pacific Technology: AI’s Hidden Backbone (13
Sep 2026)

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Asia Vital Components

Solid 3Q26 results; positive tone to 4Q26/ 
2027; reiterate Buy; trim PO to NT$3,650

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 3,650 TWD | Price: 2,790 TWD

3Q26: record-high GM/OPM for five straight quarters 
Asia Vital Components (AVC)’s 3Q26 GM/OPM of 33.9%/28.5% hit a record high for the 
fifth straight quarter, exceeding BofAe of 33.0%/27.5% and consensus of 33.3%/27.9%. 
OP (+25% QoQ, +108% YoY) was 7%/4% above BofAe/consensus, with higher sales 
(+20% QoQ, +52% YoY; 3%/2% above BofAe/consensus). We attribute the results beat 
to solid product mix (server and networking comprised 66% of 3Q26 sales, same as 1Q-
2Q26) and improving production efficiency. Despite a non-operating loss (mainly FX 
loss), NP was 10%/3% above consensus (Exhibit 3).  
Positive tone to 4Q26/2027; ongoing capacity expansion 
We expect 4Q26 sales to rise 19% QoQ and 2027 sales to grow 40% YoY, with mgmt.’s 
still positive tone to 4Q26/2027, mainly driven by AI liquid cooling projects. AVC 
indicated full support to clients’ platform upgrade and shipment pipeline. It should be 
able to pass through the higher raw material cost. In 2027, mgmt. targets ASIC sales mix 
to surpass GPU. AVC also sees solid sales momentum from chassis/rack and CDU, acting 
as the solution provider. Mgmt. maintained 2026 capex guidance at NT$15bn and guided 
NT$15-20bn for 2027 (BofAe unchanged at NT$17bn), mainly for capacity expansion in 
Vietnam. Mgmt. now targets another 50% cold plate capacity expansion in 2027. 
Trim PO to NT$3,650 for a lower P/E set-up; reiterate Buy 
We lift 2026-28E EPS by 3-10%, mainly to reflect the 3Q26 results beat and higher 
margins, driven by ongoing server/networking project ramp-up. Yet, we cut our PO to 
NT$3,650 (23x 2027E EPS), with a lower P/E multiple (2SD above average since 2020; 
Exhibit 5) from NT$3,888 (was 27x; 3SD above) on AI supply chain’s potentially higher 
shipment/pipeline uncertainty due to component shortage. Nevertheless, we reiterate 
Buy on AVC as we believe it should remain one of the key beneficiaries from rising 
liquid-cooling adoption and ongoing GPU/ASIC platform/spec upgrade. We also highlight 
solid sales/margin growth under broad product base and diversified project/client base.

Company Sector 
IT Hardware

Company Description 
AVC is the leading system-level thermal/cooling component 
maker globally. Key end markets include server, PC, 
smartphone. Key end clients include global CSPs, PC brands, 
smartphone brands. AVC was founded in 1991 and went 
publicly listed on Taiwan Stock Exchange on 27 Sep 2002. It 
is headquartered in Taiwan. Its main production sites are 
located in China (Shenzhen, Wuhan, Shanghai, Dongguan, 
Chengdu), with ongoing capacity expansion in Vietnam.

Investment Rationale 
We assign a Buy rating to AVC given its leading position in 
thermal/cooling component and total solution strategy to 
ride on strong AI market demand, double-digit sales/EPS 
CAGR in 2026-28 with rising liquid cooling sales mix and 
ASP/margin increase.

### Page 6

Price objective basis & risk 
Asia Vital Components (AVMPF) 
Our PO of NT$3,650 is based on 23x 2027E EPS. We use 23x P/E multiple (2SD above 
P/E average since 2020) in view of AVC's double-digit sales/EPS CAGR in 2026-28E. We 
believe AVC's leading position in thermal/cooling component market and strong AI 
market demand will drive rising liquid cooling sales mix and ASP/margin increase.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) stronger demand in AI server, server, PC, smartphone, (2) 
better operating leverage with larger revenue scale, (3) less intensive competition, (4) 
faster product certification, (5) faster capacity expansion, (6) favorable FX movement 
(USD/RMB and/or USD/NTD) to help on GM and/or non-operating items.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) softer AI server demand due to GPU/component 
shortage, (2) weaker server/PC/smartphone market demand amid inflation/macro 
uncertainties, (3) GM pressure due to higher material/labor/utility cost, (4) more 
intensive competition, (5) delayed product certification, (6) slower capacity expansion, 
(7) unfavorable FX movement (USD/RMB and/or USD/NTD) to weigh on GM and/or non-
operating items.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 -  Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things , 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst ’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial 
rating) 
Ratings dispersion guidelines for coverage clusterR2 
Buy ≥ 10% ≤ 70% 
Neutral ≥ 0% ≤ 30% 
Underperform N/A ≥ 20% 
R2Ratings dispersions may vary from time to time where BofA Global Research believes it better reflects the investment prospects of stocks in a Coverage Cluster. 
INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be 
secure) and 9 - pays no cash dividend.  Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a common industry, 
sector, region or other classification(s). A stock’ s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

5347 世界(Vanguard)

報 告 日 期 ：2026/11/09 研 究 員： 羅 金 鳳 (Sunny Lo ) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要：  
⚫ 4Q26 即使有產能瓶頸的限制下， 但在 AI 需 求 強 勁 的 帶 動 下 ，
營 收 及 毛 利 率 將 進 一 步 成 長。 
⚫ AI 的監管可能使產能在 AI 及非 AI 方 面 進 行 合 理 的 調 整，但 不
影響 AI 在 全 球 產 業 發 展 上 的 主 導 地 位 ， 世 界 先 進 仍 會 持 續 進 置
12 吋產能，滿足客戶需求 。 
⚫ VSMC 將於 1Q27 開始正式量產，產能將逐季上升，且都能維
持 90+% 的 產 能 利 用 率， 其 中 Interposer 將 的 營 收 貢 獻 將
>50 %。

一. 產能受 限，但  AI 需求強勁 ，4Q26 營運 續強  
受惠 AI Server 、 手 機 用 的PMIC 需 求 強 勁 ， 帶 動3Q26 整 體 晶 圓
出 貨 量 達 75.1 萬片 ，QoQ+5.33% ，YoY+11.59% ， 加 上 ASP 季
增 3%，及 有 利 的 匯 率 因 素，使 世 界 先 進3Q26 整 體 營 收 達157.02
億 元，QoQ+ 10.23 %，YoY +27.15 %，且 因 產 能 利 用 率 上 升、較 佳
的 產 品 組 合 及ASP 季增 3%， 抵 銷 生 產 成 本 的 上 升 ， 使 毛 利 率 由
2Q26 的 32.32 % 上 升 至 33.65 % ， 稅 後 淨 利 30.99 億 元 ，
QoQ+4.18 %，YoY+81.96 %，單 季 稅 後EPS 1. 65 元，優 於 預 期。

4Q26 消 費 性 產 品 需 求 疲 弱 ；車用 、 工控 相 關 的 產 品 維 持 平 穩 ；
PC/NB 受 記 憶 體 價 格 上 漲 影 響，訂 單 下 修，但 AI 需 求 維 持 強 勁 ，
訂 單 能 見 度 達4 個 月 ，即 便 在 產 能 受 限 的 影 響 下 ，產 能 利 用 率仍
將 再 由 90% 上 升 至>90% ，帶 動 晶 圓 出 貨 量QoQ+0 ～+2% ，ASP 
QoQ+3 ～+5% ，毛 利 率 將 再 上 升 至34 ～36% ，因 此，我 們 預 估 世
界 先 進 4Q26 營收 164.92 億 元，QoQ+ 5.03 %，YoY +30.96 %，毛
利率 35.03 %， 稅 後 EPS 1. 76 元。

二.AI 需求不受美國民主黨勝選影響  
就 AI 展 望 來 看 ，6 個 月 前 市 場 強 烈 看 好 到2030 年仍 處 於 供 不 應
求，美 國 期 中 選 舉 民 主 黨 大 勝後 60 小時 ，顯 示 出 許 多 政 策 需 要 進
行 調 整，其 中 AI 相 關 的 建 置 須 要 被 監 管、資 金 回 收 風 險、資 金 投
入 報 酬 率 、 電 力 不 足 等 的 擔 憂 ， 一 一 受 到 市 場 關 注 ， 世 界 先 進 認
為 市 場 可 能 會 進 行 調 整，但AI 仍是 全球 產 業 發 展 所 需，機 會 還 是
不 變 ， 只 是 之 前AI 客 戶 擔 心 拿 不 到 產 能 ， 非AI 客 戶 憂 慮 產 能 排
擠，現 在 可 能 在 產 能 的 配 置 上 會 趨 於 合 理 化，不過 AI 仍然 佔據 全
球產 業 主 要 的 地 位 ， 因 此 ， 世 界 先 進 的8 吋 廠 持 續 成 長 ， 產 能 利
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：154 元 
      20 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：120 元 
     15.56 X 2027 EPS

三.VSMC 將於 1Q27 量產， Interposer 營收占比 >50%  
世 界 先進 新 加 坡 首 座12 吋 晶 圓 廠 VSMC 產 能 受 客 戶 合 約 承 諾 ，
第 一 期 產 能 已 全 數 被 客 戶 預 定 完畢 ，06/2026 的 首批 樣本 正 式 產
出， 目 前 產 能 導 入 試 產 中 ，預估 1Q27 正 式 量 產(自 量 產 後 產 能 利
用 率 可 維 持>90%) ，逐 季 爬 坡到 產 能 滿 載，可 比 先 前 預 估 的1H29
滿 載 提 前 達 成。 因 先 進 封 裝 需 求 強 勁 ， 世 界 先 進 先 前 已 因 此 將
VSMC 的產能進行調配， Interposer 的 生 產 將 會 佔有>50% 的比
重 ， 帶 動 世 界 先 進AI 的 營 收 比 重 由2025 年的 1～3% 上 升 至 2026
年的 10+% ，2027 年在 Interposer 的 加 入 下 將 繼 續 成 長 。

四. 預估 2026/ 2027 年稅後 EPS 6.19/7.71 元 
2026 年 雖 然 車 用 及 工 控 需 求 已 觸 底 ， 但AI 仍 然 是 半 導 體 成 長 的
主 旋 律，世 界 先 進因 佈局 AI 及 資 料 中 心 相 關 的 電 源 產 品，目 前 供
不 應 求 ， 帶 動 世 界 先 進AI 相 關 的 營 收 比 重 將 由2025 年的 1～3%
上 升 至 10+ %，及 有 利 的 價 格 環 境，因 此，我 們預估 世 界 先 進2026
年 營 收 589.71 億 元 ，YoY+ 21.36 %， 毛 利 率 32. 79%， 稅 後 淨 利
116.33 億元 ，YoY+47.12 %，稅後 EPS 6.19 元 。2027 年 不 受 美
國 選 舉 影 響 ，AI 相 關 的 產 品 仍 然 供 不 應 求 ，Interposer 放 量 生 產
將 進 一 步 帶 動AI 相 關 營 收 的 貢 獻，加 上 有 利 的 價 格 環 境 下，我們
預 估 世 界 先 進2027 年 營 收 751.32 億 元 ，YoY+ 27.4 %， 毛 利 率
33.76 %，稅後 EPS 7.71 元。

五. 投資建議 為買進，目標價 154 元 
世 界 先 進 在AI 領 域 已 逐 漸 取 得 成 果，且 在AI 需 求 強 勁 的 帶 動 下，
2026 年營運 強 勁 成 長 ， 預 估2027 年在 AI 的 帶 動 及 新 加 坡12 吋
廠 進 度 優 於 預 期 ，Interposer 量 產 貢 獻 下， 產能利用率 將維持在
90+% ，且 在 有 利 的 價 格 環 境 下，有 利 獲 利 進 一 步 成 長，因 此，投
資 建 議為買 進 ， 目 標 價154 元 (20X202 7 年 稅 後 EPS) 。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
世 界 先 進(5347) 成 立於 1994 年 ， 成 立 之 初 以DRAM 製 造 為 主 ，
1999 年台 積 公 司 協 助 下，成 功 導 入 邏 輯 產 品 代 工 技 術，並 成 為 台
積 之 代 工 伙 伴。世界先進 2000 年 正 式 宣 佈由 DRAM 廠 轉 型 為 晶
圓 代 工 公 司 ，07/2004 世 界 先 進 正 式 結 束DRAM 生 產 製 造 ， 成 功
轉 型 為 百 分 之 百 的 晶 圓 代 工 公 司。 為 擴 大 營 運 規 模 ， 後 續 陸 續 於
2007 年購 入 華 邦 電 的8 吋 廠 ，2014 年 購 入 南 亞 科 技 的 八 吋 晶 圓

廠房，01/2019 併 購 格 芯 公 司 位 於 位 於 新 加 坡的 8 吋 晶 圓 廠 廠 房、

進 亦 開 始 佈 局 氮 化 鎵(GaN) 與 碳 化 矽(SiC) 產品。

二. 產業趨勢 概況  
1、 佈 局 第 三 代 半 導 體 材 料氮化鎵 (GaN)   
半 導 體 第 一 代 材 料 為 矽(Si) ， 因 價 格 相 對 便 宜 ， 製 作 成 本 相 對 較
低 ， 目 前 仍 普 偏 應 用 於 各 類 電 子 產 品 ； 第 二 代 半 導 體 材 料 為 砷 化
鎵 ， 因 其 具 有 高 頻 、 低 雜 訊 、 高 效 率 仍 低 耗 電 等 特 性 ， 廣 泛 應 用
於 通 訊 類 產 品，如 手 機 的 功 率 放 大 器(AP) ；由 於 電 動 車、5G 等相
關 應 用 要 求 高 功 率 、 高 電 壓 、 高 頻 的 特 性 ， 推 動 半 導 體 材 料 走 向
第 三 代 的 「 寬 能 隙 半 導 體 」(WBG) ，即 氮 化 鎵 (GaN) 與 碳 化 矽
(SiC) ，然 因 製 作 難 度 較Si 高 出 許 多，生 產 較 困 難，製 造 成 本 高 ，
目 前 仍 未 大 量 被 使 用 ， 不 過 其 高 功 率 、 高 電 壓 、 高 頻 、 散 熱 佳 及
可 使 終 端 產 品 做 得 更 小 的 特 性 將 是 未 來 各 廠 商 投 入 發 展 的 必 然 趨
勢。世界先進亦開始投入 氮化鎵 (GaN) 與碳化矽 (SiC) 的 開 發 ， 雖
仍 處 於 開 發 的 初 期 ，營 收 貢 獻 尚 不 明 顯， 然 我 們 認 為 此 發 展 為 長
期 趨 勢 不 變 ， 值 得 關 注 。 世 界 先 進選 擇 製 作 良 率較高 及 應 力影響
較 低 的 GaN on  QST 的 技 術，已 準 備 量 產，將 因 應 高 中 低 端 產 品 ，
第 一 代 產 品電壓 >650V ， 主 要 應 用 於 消 費 性 電 子 產 品 的 充 電 器 ，
由 於 第 三 代 半 導 體 技 術 複 雜 度 高，2024 年 出 貨僅 數 百 片，但2025
年年 成長 5～10 倍 ， 目 前以 充 電 樁 為 主 ， 未 來 將 以 應 用 於On board 
charger 為 目 標，但 因 電 壓 需 做 到1200V ，技 術難 度 更 高，技 術 開
發 所 需 的 時 間 更 長；SiC 方 面，04/2026 已 完 成 試 產，目 標 客 戶 為
IDM ， 預 估 2026 年 底 可 量 產 。

國 選 舉 影 響 ，AI 相 關 的 產 品 仍 然 供 不 應 求 ，Interposer 放 量 生 產
將 進 一 步 帶 動AI 相 關 營 收 的 貢 獻，加 上 有 利 的 價 格 環 境 下，我 們
預 估 世 界 先 進2027 年 營 收 751.32 億 元 ，YoY+27.4% ， 毛 利 率
33.76% ，稅後 EPS 7.71 元。

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성바이오로직스 (207940)

Company Brief 
제약/바이오 
Analyst 김준영 
junyoung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 김현비 
hyunbee.kim@meritz.co.kr 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요

3Q26 Review  
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(YoY +18.5%, QoQ +12.8%), 영업이익 
6,401억원(YoY +1.0%, QoQ +9.2%)으로 컨센서스를 각각 9.8%, 4.7% 상회했다. 
기존 1 ~ 4공장 풀가동 및 미국 Rockville 공장 매출 약 1,000억원과 5공장 PPQ 
물량 인식이 더해지며 외형 성장을 견인했다. 반면 신규 생산시설의 초기 비용 및 제
품 믹스 영향으로 OPM은 42.9%를 기록하며 전년 동기 대비 7.5%p 하락했다. 동
사는 연간 매출 성장률 가이던스를 기존 +15~20%에서 +20%로 상향했다. 당사는 
4분기 Rockville 공장 매출 기여와 기존 공장의 높은 가동률을 감안하여 연간 매출
액 5조 6,135억원(YoY +23.2%)을 예상한다.

신규 생산거점의 외형 확대, 이익 기여는 좀 더 긴 호흡으로 
5공장은 2Q26부터 PPQ 생산을 본격화했으나 상업 생산에 따른 실질적인 이익 기
여는 2027년 하반기에 확대될 전망이다. Rockville 공장은 3Q와 4Q 각각 약 
1,000억원의 매출이 예상되며 2027년 분기별 매출도 유사한 수준을 유지할 것으로 
전망한다. 다만 영업이익 기여까지는 시간이 더 소요될 것으로 예상되어 2027년 이
익 성장은 미국 공장보다 5공장 Ramp-up이 주도할 전망이다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스에 대해 투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원을 유지한다. 고객사 수주 
문의가 회복되는 가운데 대형 계약 협의가 진행 중이며 6공장 역시 연말 또는 내년 
초 착공을 목표로 하고 있다. 다만 신규 수주가 실제 계약으로 이어지는 시점과 5공
장의 상업 생산 전환 속도가 중기 실적의 핵심 변수로 판단한다. 추가 대형 수주 확
보 시 5공장 이후의 성장 가시성도 높아질 것으로 예상한다. 
PolyPeptide 인수는 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업을 확장한다는 점에서 긍정적이다. 인
수 완료 이후 다음 분기부터 연결 매출 인식이 가능하나 유의미한 영업이익 기여까지
는 최소 2년 이상 소요될 전망이다. PolyPeptide 인수는 현재 추정치에서 반영하지 
않았으며 향후 인수 완료 시 연결 OPM 희석 가능성을 감안할 필요가 있다. 한편 최
근 법원 항고심에서 배양부터 정제까지의 생산공정이 쟁의행위 기간에도 정상 수행되
어야 할 보전작업으로 인정되면서 파업에 따른 생산 차질 우려는 완화될 것으로 판단
한다. 노사 협상은 여전히 진행 중이나 생산 안정성에 대한 고객사 우려가 낮아질 경
우 신규 수주에도 긍정적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 22

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

Document 353: FX volatility in 2027_ In need of shock therapy

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX volatility in 2027_ In need of shock therapy.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Why is FX volatility so low? Even a 6% fall in the Nasdaq barely
budges USD/JPY these days. Have FX markets found a general
equilibrium, or is there some kind of separation between
geopolitics and macro? We think the answer lies more in the
structure of FX trading and do not rule out jump risk if the
shock is big enough

Chris Turner, Francesco Pesole and Dmitry Dolgin
Whatever happened to FX volatility?
Despite wars in the Middle East and Continental Europe, an energy-
driven inﬂation spike and growing fears over an AI bubble, FX
volatility is close to its lowest levels in over a decade. If moves in
spot exchange rates are the ultimate driver of traded volatility, then
the recent stasis in spot USD/JPY in the face of a 6% drop in the
Nasdaq tells the story. In short, if exchange rates aren’t moving,
why should the market pay up for volatility?
It is not as though FX volatility is low in all markets. AI concerns
have seen equity volatility spike, while surging energy prices,

### Page 2

central bank tightening, and fears about debt sustainability have
all seen interest rate volatility remain elevated.
FX volatility has remained muted
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
The lack of spot FX movement or spot follow-through cannot
simply be blamed on some kind of separation between geopolitics
and macro. This year’s war in Iran has driven a massive hawkish
repricing at the short end of yield curves and turned easing cycles
on their heads. And in this outlook, we argue that if there is one

theme that FX markets should take seriously, it’s global monetary
policy cycles.
Any pickup in FX volatility more likely from
equities than bonds
One of our key calls for 2027 is that we don’t expect a signiﬁcant
pickup in FX volatility. Markets have built up considerable tolerance
for US policy and geopolitical headlines, and may be even more
willing to fade US political risk if a Democrat-led Congress and
weaker Republican support constrain US President Donald Trump’s

room for aggressive policy action.
If volatility does break higher, we think equities are a more likely
trigger than rates. Historical evidence supports that view, with
equity volatility consistently showing greater pass-through into FX
than rates volatility (chart below).

### Page 3

Sticky global inﬂation and prolonged monetary tightening are
more plausible risks than a major equity selloﬀ. Yet we don’t think
even a high-inﬂation scenario would be enough to recreate 2022-
2023 levels of FX volatility. Policy rates entered the Iran war from
much higher levels than at the end of the pandemic, while ﬁscal
support is smaller and labour markets are looser. The ingredients
for another 2022-23-style rates shock simply look less potent.

Greater FX shock potential from equities
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Structural factors: Growth in ﬁnancial
assets playing a role?
When thinking about factors that could be suppressing volatility in
spot exchange rates, we suspect the growth in the asset
management industry could be playing a role. Both assets under
management and cross-border positions have continued to surge
over recent years. Using World Bank and Bank for International
Settlements (BIS) data, we now estimate that global holdings of

equities and bonds are worth around 300% of global GDP. As the BIS
reported back in 2025, FX trading volumes were about 70 times the
volume of global trade in 2025, roughly double the ratio of 1992.
These ﬁnancial FX ﬂows, including portfolio hedging and
rebalancing, can be an important source of FX liquidity when they
are gradual and diversiﬁed. But correlated hedging or rebalancing

### Page 4

can amplify FX moves. The latter was the case during the Trump
tariﬀ shock in March/April 2025.
Stock of global equity and ﬁxed income
holdings
Source: World Bank, BIS, ING
Structural factors: FX engineering playing a
role?
One of the big trends in FX trading over recent years has been the
‘internalisation’ of FX ﬂows – the concept being that a bank can
deliver steadier, better pricing for customers by oﬀsetting client
ﬂow with other internal client ﬂow/hedging activity without having

to go to external liquidity venues.
We suspect there is quite a lot of overstatement of the extent of
‘internalisation’ out there, where banks may be including dealing
on ‘lit’ venues (transparent order books) as part of their
internalisation activity. Undoubtedly, the advent of non-bank
liquidity providers to lit venues has been a major contributor to the
depth of market pricing, as has the growth of algorithmic pricing
engines. The design of these algo pricing engines, which pull back

from putting large volumes through in thin conditions, has
probably contributed to calmer conditions, too. Indeed, it seems
that when volatility conditions pick up, the buy-side prefers to use
algos to execute rather than paying the wider spreads associated
with principal liquidity transfers.

But before we conclude that FX volatility is dead, evidence shows
that major events – Liberation Day in April 2025, or the start of the
Iran war in February 2026 – can still have a big say in FX markets.
Below, we show some data on the depth of the EUR/USD market
starting in 2025. The chart shows the impact on EUR/USD, in USD

pips, one minute after a trade has gone through. We show the
impact of four size buckets, equally weighted: $0-1m, $1-5m, $5-
10m and $10-30m.
Big events do still impact market depth, which could be due to
some non-banks pulling liquidity from lit venues.
So, while the above factors could be contributing to lower volatility,
our conclusion is that volatility is not dead and jump risk or fat tails
will still be a fact of life in FX markets.

This Month in Geopolitics highlights key events, issues, and themes for the month ahead. The map below provides an at-a-glance summary, with detailed
analysis provided in the subsequent slides. For a longer-term outlook (November 2026 – January 2027), please refer to our latest 90-day Geopolitical Risk Radar.

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
US-China talks, a possible Trump-Xi-Putin trilateral meeting, and any signs of a China-Japan diplomatic
thaw will be critical watchpoints. The priorities of the summit between representatives of 21 Asia-Pacific
economies are set to include efforts to boost innovation, digital connectivity, supply-chain resilience and

support for an open multilateral trading system. During the summit, presidents Trump and Xi are expected to
meet for the third time this year. This meeting will provide signals on the state of China-US talks, including the
operationalisation of new bilateral trade and investment boards, and on the likelihood of another extension of
the Busan trade truce beyond January 2027. President Trump has also indicated he is open to a trilateral US-
China-Russia meeting during the summit, possibly covering nuclear arms control and the Russia-Ukraine war.

Investors will also closely watch whether Japanese Prime Minister Takaichi and President Xi meet on the
sidelines, following reports that Tokyo is hoping to arrange a meeting. However, the bar for a formal
breakthrough will likely remain high. As we noted in a report earlier this year, a full normalisation of relations
and an easing of Chinese economic restrictions – which include rare-earth export controls – may require a
lengthy period of diplomatic re-engagement and backchannel diplomacy.

A divided government could mean less change at home but more abroad. Third-party polling and betting
markets forecast Democrat control of the House, and an increasing probability of flipping the Senate. 80% of
respondents in our monthly poll now say living costs will influence their vote – the most since April. For
markets, a divided government could mean less domestic legislative change, but not necessarily a more
predictable US foreign policy. Losing Congress could leave theadministration’s geopolitical and geoeconomic

toolkit largely intact.
In our view, weapons stockpiles, inflationary pressures and long-end yields may matter more than Congress
in constraining theadministration’s choices in 2027 and 2028. A Democratic House could dial up oversight of
the administration, launch investigations, force votes on War Powers Resolutions, and use appropriations bills
to press for restrictions on departmental funding. This may generate a steady stream of attention-grabbing

headlines, but whether it decisively affects the event-driven risk that matters for markets (e.g. blocking a tariff
or preventing a limited military strike) remains in doubt. If domestic policymaking becomes more challenging,
foreign policy could become a top priority for the Trump administration.

### Page 4

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
NewZealand’s general election is shaping up as a close and fragmented contest. Both the ruling National
and opposition Labour parties are polling at historically low levels, and smaller parties will likely determine
the next government. In a closely contested vote, investors could see coalition uncertainty and a wider

range of possible outcomes for tax, spending, infrastructure, energy and migration policy.
The National party is campaigning on a platform that would broadly preserve the current emphasis on
fiscal restraint, lower regulation and private-sector investment. A Labour-led government could place
greater weight on public services, housing and climate policy, alongside a proposed capital-gains tax on
investment property. However, because any government will rely on minor parties for survival, the final

policy mix will depend heavily on post-election negotiations.
The meeting will be a test of the EU’s ability to translate the competitiveness agenda into practical
reforms. The focus will be on Europe’s ability to restore competitiveness, mobilize investment and
maintain fiscal discipline. The Eurogroup brings together Euro-area finance ministers and is expected to
consider macroeconomic and fiscal developments, energy market pressures and progress towards the

Savings and Investments Union. ECOFIN brings together all EU finance ministers, and will focus on
financial integration, banking sector competitiveness, and economic coordination. To watch will be
tensions between fiscal consolidation and the need to fund defence, energy security and strategic
investment, alongside progress on integratingEurope’s fragmented capital markets and banking sectors. A
budget-focused Council meeting follows on 10 November.

Implementation of existing commitments, rather than new policy pledges, is expected to be the primary
focus of the next climate summit in Antalya. Building on the shift towards implementation at COP30,
COP31 will focus on translating existing climate commitments into practical action, including through
investment, financing and partnerships. For investors, the key watchpoint will be whether the summit can
deliver tangible commitments that translate existing plans into binding funding pledges for critical

infrastructure resilience, scaled-up renewable energy output, and climate finance.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in November 2026

### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
Melaka’s state election will offer a further gauge of Malaysia’s evolving electoral landscape after
strong recent state-level performances by Barisan Nasional (BN). The contest will also be watched for
its read-through to Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim’s Pakatan Harapan (PH), following setbacks in other

state elections this year.
BN enters the election from a position of relative strength, while its electoral understanding with PAS
and its allies could provide an early indication of how political alignments are evolving ahead of the next
general election. However, Melaka’s local dynamics mean the result should not be treated as a direct
national forecast.
The anticipated signing of a landmark digital economy agreement is set to be one of the most

significant summit deliverables in years. The 49th ASEAN Summit in Manila will focus on economic
integration, energy security, supply-chain resilience, maritime security, the crisis in Myanmar, and
relations with external partners.
For markets, the formal signing of the ASEAN Digital Economy Framework Agreement (DEFA) is the key
event to watch. Designed as a legally binding treaty, DEFA aims to harmonize regional digital trade rules
and cross-border data flows. Successful implementation is expected to double the projected size of the

region’s digital economy by 2030, boosting it from a projected baseline of US$1 trillion to US$2 trillion
(up from approximately US$300 billion today).
The second EU-UK Summit will be an important test of whether the post-Brexit relationship can move
towards tangible outcomes. Prime Minister Andy Burnham will seek to finalize agreements on food and
agricultural standards, youth mobility, and linking emissions trading systems. Burnham has framed
relations as “not good enough.” To watch will be whether we see concrete deals versus declarations, and

whether leaders are prepared to make the concessions necessary to deepen ties.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in November 2026
Listed in chronological order
49th ASEAN Summit (16-18 November)

### Page 6

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
The upcoming local elections could serve as a political barometer ahead of the 2028 general election–
but only up to a point. Local elections in Taiwan are primarily driven by municipal ‘bread and butter’
economic issues; geopolitics and cross-Strait relations have historically not been at the forefront of

voters’ concerns. While this divergence limits theelections’ predictive value for the January 2028 general
vote, the results can provide a sense of the domestic balance of power.
Elections in Taiwan’s six special municipalities – Taipei, New Taipei, Taichung, Tainan, Taoyuan, and
Kaohsiung – will serve as particularly important watchpoints. Some recent polling suggests a potentially
close contest in Taipei between incumbent mayor Chiang Wan-an (Kuomintang) and legislator Shen Po-

yang (Democratic Progressive Party).
Spain’s snap general election could mark a major turning point for Spanish politics and the EU,
potentially ending eight years of Socialist-led government under Pedro Sánchez and bringing the
conservative People’s Party (PP) to power with support from the far-right Vox. Polling suggests PP is
ahead and a PP-Vox alignment is the only one projected to reach a majority. A rightward shift in one of
the EU’s largest economies could occur just as Europe enters a wider electoral cycle, potentially

complicating consensus on migration, climate policy, Ukraine and European integration.
The Forum comes at a pivotal moment in EU-Latin American relations. It is the EU’s first big commercial
push into Argentina since the EU-Mercosur trade deal began to be provisionally applied on 1 May. The
agreement opens new opportunities for trade, investment and market access. The EU is Argentina’s
largest source of foreign direct investment. Around 800 government, business and financial leaders will

focus on energy, critical minerals, agriculture and infrastructure. The EU’s Global Gateway investment
agenda provides a framework for mobilizing public and private capital. While a key outcome will be the
launch of a structured EU-Argentina investment dialogue, to watch will be the ability of Argentine
officials to allay concerns around policy stability and regulatory predictability.

### Page 7

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
A potential Trump-Kim meeting in November could restart official bilateral dialogue, though North
Korea remains highly unlikely to abandon its nuclear program. South Korea’s National Intelligence
Service said in late September that it saw a roughly 50% chance of US-North Korea talks around the
APEC Summit in Shenzhen, although more recent reporting suggests the prospects may have

diminished. If talks do resume, they would only mark the first step in a long and highly uncertain
diplomatic process.
As we noted earlier this year, North Korea is highly unlikely to agree to denuclearization. Unless renewed
negotiations sidestep denuclearization in favor of a risk-reduction or coexistence framework, dialogue is
likely to stall, not least given Pyongyang’s constitutional pledge to continue expanding its nuclear
arsenal. This would make even a negotiated freeze on further expansion difficult to achieve.

Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 27) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

The Foundation and Foundation+ models are allocations that you can use as the starting point for building a diversified 
investment portfolio. The Foundation model showcases a set of allocations focusing on traditional asset classes that are 
accessible to most investors, while the Found ation+ model includes allocations to private assets that may be accessible to 
investors in some jurisdictions, but not others.

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027 , as AI-led expansion continues.  The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4 %, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2. 3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026 , but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risk s 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures ( PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees c ore PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2. 4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates  at 4.0% , after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8 
ECB to hike once more  in December, then pause . The 
Euro area recovery is firming. GDP grew 1.2% y/y in Q2 2026, 
up from 0.6% in Q1, and the manufacturing PMI stayed robust 
at 52.4 in October. Consensus 2026 growth has been raised 
to 1.0% from 0.9%, and 2027 is unchanged at 1.3%. The main 
supports are resilient exports and German infrastructure and 
defence spending. However, cautious consumers and soft 
private investment limit the recovery, and energy costs are 
squeezing household incomes and business margins. 
Recession is not the central expectation. French fiscal stress 
and sustained high energy costs remain the key risks. 
Higher energy prices pushed Euro area headline inflation up 
to 3.8% y/y in September from 3.2% y/y in August, while core 
inflation rose to 2.5%  y/y. Consensus expects headline and 
core inflation to ease to 2% y/y and 2.4% y/y by Q4 2027, 
respectively. Encouragingly, energy has had limited impact on 
core prices, and longer -term inflation expectations remain 
stable at around 2.1%. Together, these should allow inflation 
to moderate in 2027. The main risk is that energy , and 
especially gas, prices stay high for longer than expected. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.5% in October after raising 
it by 50bps this year . Inflation data continue to favour an 
additional hike in December. We expect an ECB rate hold in 
H1 2027, followed by a cut by Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This policy path is more dovish than money markets , which 
are pricing more than 60bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
China to provide targeted support to defend growth floor. 
China's growth momentum recovered in September. Both the 
manufacturing PMI (50.1) and the non -manufacturing PMI 
(50.2) moved back into expansion. The construction PMI rose 
above 50 for the first time this year as infrastructure spending 
started to take effect. Exports grew 36.3% y/y, pushing the 
trade surplus to a record USD  133.5bn. We expect growth in 
2026 to meet the lower end of the 4.5-5.0% target. Growth is 
increasingly bifurcated, though , with  technology firms and 
exporters doing well, while household demand stays weak. 
Prices are rising again, but only narrowly. Consumer inflation 
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, and producer inflation 
surged to 5% y/y, its highest since 2022, driven by energy and 
metals prices and concentrated in upstream sectors. With 
domestic demand weak and a high comparison base next 
year, the boost from energy prices is likely to fade in 2027. 
Policy is in ‘targeted easing’ mode. We expect a modest fiscal 
package to defend the growth floor rather than a large 
stimulus. The PBoC cut a key  rate by 25bps to 1.5% to fund 
‘Six Networks’ infrastructure projects and raised its relending 
quotas. A new interest subsidy on first -home mortgages 
provides limited support to demand. We expect no policy rate 
cut this year. There is still room to speed up fiscal spending, 
including by using CNY 500bn of unused local -government 
debt quota. If the PBoC switches to an overnight reverse repo 
rate as its policy rate, that would imply a de facto rate cut as 
the overnight rate is lower than the 7-day official policy rate. 
BoJ to hike at a  quarterly pace  until Q2 2027.  Japan's 
economy remains solid. Wages are growing above 4% , and 
the labour market is tight. The main headwind is high energy 
prices, which is slowing growth. Fiscal spending gives some 
support: the FY27 draft budget includes ambitious spending 
plans, alongside a proposed two-year cut to the consumption 
tax on food and a new five -year defence programme. 
However, this extra spending raises questions about fiscal 
sustainability. The BoJ unveiled plans to triple the pace of ETF 
sales from January 2027, potentially funding the tax cut. 
Price pressures now look firmly established. Headline and 
core inflation rose above 2% for the first time this year, and 
core-core inflation (ex-fresh food and energy) jumped to 2.4% 
and is expected to rise towards 3%  in Q1 2027 . Breakeven 
inflation rates have stayed above 2% most of this year. Real 
interest rates remain negative, hurting savers and pensioners. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.25% in October, after raising 
it in September, only three months after its previous hike. This 
points to an accelerated quarterly hiking pace. We expect the 
quarterly pace of hikes to continue, taking the rate to 2% by 
June 2027. Money markets have caught up with our hawkish 
view in recent months. The BoJ noted for the first time that 
underlying inflation has reached its 2% target, marking a shift 
from creating inflation to containing it. Politics could slow the 
hiking cycle  in H2 2027. Two government -appointed board 
members voted against the September hike, and the two most 
hawkish members are due to leave in July 2027.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Our view

Foundation: Bond yields remain near multi-year highs, offering an attractive entry point. We view fixed income as a Core holding 
(ie, a Neutral allocation) in our multi-asset portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an Overweight stance on EM USD 
government bonds, given their attractive risk-reward. We hold an  Underweight view on Developed Market ( DM) government 
bonds due to the tail risks associated with term premia. 
The Fed’s decision to hold rates at 3.75-4.00% in October amid still-elevated energy prices suggests that every Fed meeting 
from here is ‘live’, with a rate change possible on incoming data . With US inflation still running hot and the labour market tight, 
we expect two more 25bps  hikes (one by year-end and one in H1 2027), bringing the Fed’s target range for the federal funds 
rate to 4.25-4.5%. We continue to favour the 3 -7-year part of the  US government bond yield curve as the best hedge against 
any risk-off move driven by AI-related growth fears. As inflation pressures ease in H2 2027, we see room for the Fed to consider 
a rate cut, which should pull nominal yields lower. Solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep spreads 
tight, but there is limited room for them to tighten further. We still favour EM USD over local-currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, broad US HYs and Australian dollar (AUD) corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We remain Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, 
government bond yields have retraced meaningfully after a 
sharp bear steepening through September and early October. 
We expect real yield s to stay elevated over the next six 
months as AI capex and productivity growth continue to drive 
above-trend economic growth. We expect two more 25bps 
rate hikes by June 2027.  
Over the next few months, we expect US government bond 
yields to remain rangebound. The near-term path hinges on 
five factors: 1) the 3 November US mid-term election, where 
a ‘Democratic sweep’ would likely be bond -supportive by 
constraining US President Trump’s fiscal spending agenda , 
while a ‘Republican sweep’ would likely weigh on bonds as 
markets price in further fiscal expansion and a higher term 
premium; 2) Fed communication at upcoming policy meetings; 
3) the US Treasury’s quarterly refunding announcement on 4 
November, where a ny signal of reduced long -end issuance  
would likely compress long-end yields; 4) softening auction 
demand and repatriation by foreign investors, which add 
uncertainty around demand for US government bonds; and 5) 
the f luid geopolitical situation in the Middle East , which is 
keeping oil prices elevated, with a risk of further upside. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect moderating oil prices and the 
fading inflationary impact of tariffs to give the Fed room to 
consider rate cuts. The US 30-year yield, however, is likely to 
stay elevated as fiscal deficit remains substantial next year.  
We expect a final ECB hike to 2.75% by December 2026, then 
a pause in H1 2027, followed by a 25bps cut in Q3 2027. In 
Japan, we expect quarterly pace of BoJ hikes, with one hike 
that takes the policy rate to 1.50% by December 2026 and two 
more in H1 2027, reaching 2.00% before pausing at mid-year.

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10 
DM corporates – Core holding 
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across DM credit. Credit spreads have widened from 
their lows but remain close to historical tights, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight-spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion,  
especially in the IG space, where heavy AI-related issuance 
is weighing on the long end of the curve. AI -driven supply is 
surpassing USD 2 50bn in 2026 and mak ing up a growing 
share of US IG gross issuance, but there are growing signs 
that hyperscalers are turning to private markets for fundin g, 
which should help ease public bond market supply pressures.  
We remain selective at the long end  of the yield curve and  
favour a duration of  3-7 years, where risk -adjusted carry 
remains most durable. Issuance-driven concessions in 
sectors with strong capex cycles (notably technology, capital 
goods and basic industries) present opportunities for investors 
who can stomach near-term volatility. 
Fig. 12 DM IG spreads have widened from their lows, 
but remain near historically tight levels 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
DM HY spreads  have widened to roughly a six-month high. 
Persistent doubts about whether hyperscalers can generate 
adequate returns on massive AI capex investments triggered 
a broad risk asset sell-off. The CCC tier has become the focal 
point of genuine credit concern, with yields hovering near the 
high-15% range and spreads near a four-year high. Market 
focus has shifted to the funding pressures facing AI-related 
neocloud companies. We view this  as idiosyncratic for now 
and see a limited impact on the broader HY universe.  The 
underlying credit picture is mixed. Leverage is elevated and 
interest coverage is thin, but the broad market has not yet 
shown signs of systemic stress. The US HY d efault rate  
remains below trend in 2026. 
HY offers an attractive income proposition, with all-in yields of 
over 8%. Its shorter-duration profile, combined with its lower 
sensitivity to changes in the term premium and rate volatility, 
make it an attractive sleeve in the current phase of the cycle.  
We remain attentive to evolving credit rating migration trends 
and sector-specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades 
slow and new issuance clusters around key themes. However, 
healthy issuer fu ndamentals and stable credit metrics  
continue to support it as a Core holding allocation. 
Fig. 13 DM HY spreads are off the tights 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight 
USD/Core holding for LCY 
We are Overweight EM USD government bonds and retain 
EM LCY government bonds as a Core holding, reflecting a 
balanced perspective across both regional performance and 
currency dynamics. In EM USD sovereign debt, the recent 
spread widening offered an attractive entry point to gain 
exposure to areas benefiting from resilient global growth and 
stable external conditions. Technicals remain favourable, with 
supply being front-loaded and investor positioning remaining 
relatively light compared with historical levels. 
Many EM central banks having tightened earlier and more 
aggressively post -pandemic, preserving credibility and 
maintaining positive real rates. External balances have 
improved, reserve buffers have increased and greater 
reliance on domestic funding reduces external vulnerability. 
Lula’s surprise win in Brazil’s presidential election raises 
political risk. The country’s fiscal trajectory under the incoming 
administration is a key risk to monitor. Fiscal slippage could  
trigger a broader repricing of LATAM risk. In Asia, higher-rated 
issuers continue to offer a valuable buffer during bouts of 
global rate volatility, while select frontier exposures in EMEA 
remain more sensitive to liquidity shifts and changes in risk 
appetite. Index composition also shapes resilience: the EM 
USD benchmark is less exposed to oil price shocks , as 
countries that are not oil importers account for a larger share 
of the benchmark than in  the LCY benchmark. We expect 
returns to be primarily driven by carry rather than further 
spread compression.

Global Market Outlook 11 
Fig. 14 EM USD government bonds offer a yield pick-up 
of nearly 3ppt over DM peers 
Bloomberg EM USD Government Bond Index, yield to worst

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM LCY debt faces additional headwinds, especially as 
elevated US real yields and an appreciating USD dampen 
demand from foreign investors and reduce the comparative 
attractiveness of broad -based duration strategies. However, 
real yields remain comparatively supportive in several 
markets, and current valuations signal selective opportunities, 
especially given the restrictive monetary policy stances 
prevalent across many EM economies. Accordingly, EM LCY 
bonds retain their Core holding status, with portfolio emphasis 
placed on country selection and measured duration exposure, 
reflecting the differentiated currency dynamics at play. 
Key risks to both  the USD and LCY segments include a 
potential escalation of the  Middle East conflict , higher oil 
prices, renewed hawkishness from the Fed that could support 
further USD strength and weather-related disruptions, such as 
the effects of El Niño . Climate risks could prolong inflation 
pressures and hinder disinflation in vulnerable EM regions. 
Asia USD bonds – Core holding 
We retain a Neutral allocation to Asia USD bonds, an asset 
class that continues to reward investors with attractive 
nominal yields, favourable supply -demand technicals and 
robust credit fundamentals. Healthy corporate cash flows, low 
leverage and a high share of sovereign-linked issuers lend the 
segment a defensive quality relative to broader credit. 
The resilience has been evident year to date (YTD). Asia USD 
bonds have outperformed  both DM government and DM IG 
corporate bonds  on a total -return basis. We expect this 
relative strength to persist. The combination of carry and 
credit quality (over 60% of issues are sovereign or sovereign-
related) anchors our conviction in the asset class, even as we 
maintain a Neutral stance.

Bond opportunistic views 
Bullish US HY bonds 
We are opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. We expect 
HY corporate earnings and cash flows to remain solid and 
leverage to remain stable. US HY bonds’  shorter-duration 
profile offers lower sensitivity to interest-rate volatility, which 
is an attractive characteristic in an environment of elevated 
term premia. 
Bullish US inflation-protected bonds (TIPS) 
Real yields remain elevated versus historical standards , 
driven by rate hike expectations. Inflation concerns persist as 
energy prices remain subject to upside risks. We believe TIPS 
offer protection against near-term inflation uncertainty.   
Bullish AUD corporate bonds 
The threshold for further RBA hikes has risen, as higher 
energy prices weigh on the growth outlook and complicate the 
policy trade-off. The transmission channel matters too, where 
higher long -end yields could feed through to the property 
market, tightening financial conditions without the RBA having 
to act, casting further doubt on the case for additional hikes. 
Technicals have improved slightly as higher yields slow new 
supply. Supply remains a tail risk, though. The AUD bond

market is much smaller than the USD market, especially at the 
long end of the curve, where demand for duration is thinner 
and the term premium is most sensitive to supply. 
Bullish AAA-rated CLOs 
Concerns about spillovers from p rivate credit are mounting 
amid deteriorating covenant protections in parts of the 
leveraged credit market. However, we believe AAA-rated 
CLOs backed by solid asset portfolios and substantial credit 
enhancements underneath senior tranches should help 
dampen credit risk and volatility. We see a compelling case 
for floating-rate and shorter -duration alternatives as portfolio 
diversifiers amid elevated term premia.  
Close utilities corporate hybrids 
AI capex is set to keep rising, but funding it has become 
harder. Tighter financial conditions now coincide with growing 
competition for capital. Utilities' credit fundamentals remain 
strong. Even so, we close our utilities corporate hybrids idea 
to lock in a gain of 0.5% as a risk-management step.

### Page 12

Global Market Outlook 12

Our view

We downgrade global equities from Overweight to a Core allocation as we expect higher volatility in the near term. We 
expect this volatility to arise as equity market leadership broadens during the transition to a mid-cycle bull market. Equity returns 
will likely moderate from the 15-20% annualised range of the last few years but remain positive. We remain Overweight US and 
AxJ equities, which are expected to deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 among the major regions. 
Within AxJ, we remain Overweight Taiwan, given structural AI-driven growth, and upgrade India to Overweight, for diversified 
non-AI growth exposure. South Korea is a Core holding, with attractive valuations but extreme volatility . China is also a Core 
holding, with attractive valuations but lacking a positive catalyst. We are Underweight ASEAN, which has a weaker growth profile. 
We maintain Japan as a Core holding as nominal growth supports broad-based earnings revisions. We also maintain a Core 
allocation to Europe ex-UK, which is enjoying an earnings recovery. We remain Underweight UK equities, given the market’s 
limited exposure to growth sectors.
